Read this first — honesty limits.

  • Maturity labels are plain-words labels from the universal research maturity scale (E1 Established, E2 Reproduced, E3 Engineering-ready, E4 Experimental, E5 Prototype, E6 Integrated, E7 Operational; T1 Theoretical, T2 Hypothetical, T3 Speculative; U Unknown). A label says how mature a technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.
  • Any dates, horizons, or timelines here are PLANNING WINDOWS, never predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs.
  • Public records are documented evidence, never established fact. Signature records are design-study, never presented as authentic public record.
  • No consciousness or sentience claim is made here. Machine consciousness is theories and testable models only, never an established fact; no claim is made that a machine is conscious, sentient, or aware.
  • Climate restoration records are research only; no proven-intervention claims are made.
  • Post-quantum records are defensive security only.
  • Synthetic biology is design-study only, with biosafety screening; no pathogen, toxin, gene-drive, or synthesis-enabling content appears here.

This website is the subject website for the Artificial Photosynthesis Database database, public edition. It holds the full database: all 24 records, each with its description, math proof, science proof, history, construction workflow, generator, AI, demo, and sources.

1. Descriptions

Terms in Plain Words

database: a database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to. record: a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. slug: a slug is the short web-address name of a database, written with lower-case words joined by hyphens. manifest: the manifest is the master list of all databases in the network, with each database's slug, record kind, identifier prefix, and record count. record kind: a record kind is the label that says what sort of entry a record is, for example a future advanced energy storage record. identifier prefix: an identifier prefix is the fixed letters at the start of every record identifier in one database, for example JAH-ADES-. generator: a generator is a program that creates new records or files on demand from a starting number called a seed, instead of storing every output in advance. seed: a seed is a starting number fed to a generator so that the same seed always produces the same output. generator gate: a generator gate is a check a generator must pass before its output is accepted, for example the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind. JCC code: a JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, is a short code that points to one exact record, written as JAHDB followed by the database slug, a hash sign, and the record identifier. API: an API, which stands for Application Programming Interface, is a defined way for one program to ask another program for data or work. MCP: an MCP, which stands for Model Context Protocol, is a plug that lets an AI (artificial intelligence) program read a database directly. AI: AI stands for artificial intelligence, which is a program that performs tasks that usually need human thought, such as answering, classifying, or generating. SQLite: SQLite is a small database engine that stores a whole database in one file on disk. SQL: SQL, which stands for Structured Query Language, is the language used to ask questions of a relational database. JSON: JSON, which stands for JavaScript Object Notation, is a plain-text format for storing structured data with named fields. HTML: HTML, which stands for HyperText Markup Language, is the language that marks up the structure of a web page. CSS: CSS, which stands for Cascading Style Sheets, is the language that describes how a web page looks. URL: a URL, which stands for Uniform Resource Locator, is the full web address of a page or file. HTTP: HTTP, which stands for HyperText Transfer Protocol, is the set of rules a browser uses to fetch a page from a web server. HTTPS: HTTPS, which stands for HyperText Transfer Protocol Secure, is HTTP sent over a protected connection. NIST: NIST stands for the National Institute of Standards and Technology, the United States measurement and standards agency that runs the post-quantum cryptography standardization project. QKD: QKD stands for Quantum Key Distribution, a way for two parties to share a secret key using quantum physics so that eavesdropping can be detected. PQC: PQC stands for Post-Quantum Cryptography, encryption designed to stay secure even against a future quantum computer. DOE: DOE stands for the Department of Energy, the United States government department for energy research and policy. test suite: a test suite is a collection of automated tests that check whether a program behaves as specified. demo: a demo, short for demonstration, is a small working program that runs and shows its result. website interface: a website interface is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses a system. JAH: JAH is the initials of Justin Addam Higgins, the maker name used across the network. design-study: a design-study is a Signature-made design, an invented proposal that is labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. UNREACHABLE: UNREACHABLE is the label recorded for a resource whose fetch failed, together with the exact reason the fetch returned. maturity label: a maturity label is a short code saying how mature a technology is: E1 Established, E2 Reproduced, E3 Engineering-ready, E4 Experimental, E5 Prototype, E6 Integrated, E7 Operational, T1 Theoretical, T2 Hypothetical, T3 Speculative, and U Unknown. research question: a research question is the single clear question a research project sets out to answer, written before any work begins. prototype: a prototype is an early working model built to test whether an idea works in practice, before any finished product is made. simulation: a simulation is a computer model that imitates a real system so its behaviour can be tested without building it. validation: validation is the work of checking, by running tests and comparing against evidence, that a design does what its specification says it does. evidence label: an evidence label says what kind of evidence a record rests on: documented means built from fetched public records, and design-study means a Signature-made design. battery: a battery is a device that stores energy as chemical energy and gives it back as electricity when it is used. anode: an anode is the part of a battery where electric current flows out into the circuit when the battery is giving power. cathode: a cathode is the part of a battery where electric current flows in from the circuit when the battery is giving power. electrolyte: an electrolyte is the material inside a battery that lets charged particles move between the anode and the cathode while keeping the two apart. energy density: energy density is how much energy a storage device holds for its weight or its size, usually stated in watt-hours per kilogram. watt-hour: a watt-hour is a unit of energy: one watt of power used for one hour, and a kilowatt-hour is one thousand watt-hours. charge cycle: a charge cycle is one full charge and one full discharge of a battery, and a battery is rated for how many cycles it can survive before it wears out. round-trip efficiency: round-trip efficiency is the share of the energy put into a storage device that can be got back out again, stated as a percentage. grid: the grid is the network of wires, power stations, and substations that carries electricity from where it is made to where it is used. solid-state battery: a solid-state battery is a battery whose electrolyte is a solid material instead of a liquid, which can make it safer and able to hold more energy. flow battery: a flow battery is a battery that stores its energy in liquid chemicals held in tanks, so its capacity grows by using bigger tanks. supercapacitor: a supercapacitor is a storage device that holds electric charge directly on surfaces, charging and discharging very fast but holding less energy than a battery of the same size. lithium-ion: lithium-ion means a battery type that moves lithium ions, which are charged lithium atoms, through its electrolyte to store and release energy. state of charge: state of charge is how full a battery is, stated as a percentage from empty to full. thermal runaway: thermal runaway is a failure in which a battery heats itself faster than it can cool, which can lead to fire, and it is the main safety failure battery designs must prevent. photosynthesis: photosynthesis is the process green plants use to turn sunlight, water, and carbon dioxide into sugars and oxygen. artificial photosynthesis: artificial photosynthesis is a human-made system that copies the plant process, using sunlight to split water or to turn carbon dioxide into a fuel. photocatalyst: a photocatalyst is a material that speeds up a chemical change when light falls on it, without being used up itself. catalyst: a catalyst is a material that speeds up a chemical change without being used up itself. water splitting: water splitting is the chemical change that breaks water into hydrogen and oxygen using energy, here energy from sunlight. hydrogen: hydrogen is the lightest chemical element, a gas that can be burned or used in a fuel cell to release energy, leaving water behind. oxygen: oxygen is a gas in the air that living things breathe and that is released when water is split. carbon dioxide: carbon dioxide is a gas in the air, made when fuels burn, that artificial photosynthesis can turn into a fuel instead of letting it build up. solar fuel: a solar fuel is a fuel made using sunlight, such as hydrogen made by splitting water with sunlight, that can be stored and used later. photoelectrochemical cell: a photoelectrochemical cell is a device that uses light falling on a material in a liquid to drive a chemical change, such as splitting water. semiconductor: a semiconductor is a material that can absorb light and turn it into moving electric charges, and it is the light-catching part of most solar devices. light absorber: a light absorber is the part of a device that catches sunlight and turns it into electric charges that can do chemical work. electron: an electron is a tiny particle with a negative electric charge, and moving electrons are what electric current is made of. proton: a proton is a tiny particle with a positive electric charge found in the nucleus, which is the centre, of an atom. artificial leaf: an artificial leaf is a thin device shaped like a leaf that uses sunlight to split water into hydrogen and oxygen. qubit: a qubit, short for quantum bit, is the basic unit of quantum information, like a bit in an ordinary computer but able to hold a blend of zero and one at once. quantum bit: a quantum bit is the same as a qubit: the basic unit of quantum information, able to hold a blend of zero and one at once. superposition: superposition is the quantum property by which a qubit can hold a blend of zero and one at the same time until it is measured. entanglement: entanglement is the quantum property by which two qubits can be linked so that measuring one at once fixes the state of the other, however far apart they are. decoherence: decoherence is the loss of a quantum state when the qubit is disturbed by its surroundings, such as heat or stray electric fields. quantum error correction: quantum error correction is a set of methods that spread one qubit of information across many physical qubits so that errors can be found and fixed. quantum key distribution: quantum key distribution is a way for two parties to share a secret key using quantum physics so that anyone listening in can be detected. quantum network: a quantum network is a set of connected quantum devices that can share entanglement and send quantum information between distant places. quantum computer: a quantum computer is a computer that stores and processes information in qubits and uses quantum rules such as superposition and entanglement. quantum gate: a quantum gate is a basic operation that changes the state of one or more qubits, like a step in a quantum calculation. measurement: measurement is the act of reading a qubit, which forces its blended state to settle into a definite zero or one. post-quantum cryptography: post-quantum cryptography is encryption designed to stay secure even against a future quantum computer. quantum repeater: a quantum repeater is a device that extends a quantum network over long distances by refreshing entanglement partway along the path. quantum sensing: quantum sensing is measuring with quantum devices, such as using qubits to detect tiny magnetic fields or tiny changes in time.

What Artificial Photosynthesis Is

JAH-APHO-000001 · artificial photosynthesis identity · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Artificial Photosynthesis Database, catalog item 20 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is What Artificial Photosynthesis Is. Artificial photosynthesis is a human-made system that copies the plant process, using sunlight to split water or to turn carbon dioxide into a fuel. A plant uses sunlight, water, and carbon dioxide to make sugars and oxygen; an artificial system uses sunlight, water, and carbon dioxide to make hydrogen or a carbon-based fuel that can be stored and used later. The work is experimental: devices exist in laboratories, but none yet runs at the scale and cost of a working fuel plant. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The system identity is fixed: this is a future artificial photosynthesis record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established practice, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The research question this record answers is: How can sunlight be turned into a storable fuel as reliably as a plant turns it into sugar? Natural Photosynthesis as the Model

JAH-APHO-000002 · natural photosynthesis as the model · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Artificial Photosynthesis Database, catalog item 20 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Natural Photosynthesis as the Model. Natural photosynthesis is the process green plants use to turn sunlight, water, and carbon dioxide into sugars and oxygen, and it is the model every artificial system copies. Plants split water to release oxygen, move electrons through a chain of proteins, and use that energy to fix carbon dioxide into sugars. The plant's tricks worth copying are its use of abundant materials, its repair of damaged parts, and its working at ordinary temperature and pressure. Its limits worth beating are its low overall efficiency and its slow speed. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The research question this record answers is: Which tricks of the plant are worth copying, and which of its limits must an artificial system beat? Water Splitting for Hydrogen

JAH-APHO-000003 · water splitting for hydrogen · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Artificial Photosynthesis Database, catalog item 20 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Water Splitting for Hydrogen. Water splitting is the chemical change that breaks water into hydrogen and oxygen using energy, here energy from sunlight. Hydrogen is the lightest chemical element, a gas that can be burned or used in a fuel cell to release energy, leaving water behind. A water-splitting device must do two hard jobs at once: catch sunlight efficiently, and drive the chemistry fast enough to matter, without its materials wearing out in water and light. This record specifies the research: light absorbers, catalysts for each half of the reaction, and the cell that holds them together. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The research question this record answers is: How can a device split water with sunlight efficiently enough, and for long enough, to matter? Carbon Dioxide Reduction to Fuels

JAH-APHO-000004 · carbon dioxide reduction to fuels · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Artificial Photosynthesis Database, catalog item 20 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Carbon Dioxide Reduction to Fuels. Carbon dioxide reduction is the chemical change that turns carbon dioxide, the gas in the air made when fuels burn, into a fuel such as carbon monoxide, methanol, or methane, using energy from sunlight. It is harder than water splitting, because the reaction has many possible products and the device must choose the wanted one. This record specifies the research: catalysts that select one product, the energy cost of each step, and the measurement that proves which fuel was actually made and how much of it. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. Photocatalysts and Light Absorbers

JAH-APHO-000005 · photocatalysts and light absorbers · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Artificial Photosynthesis Database, catalog item 20 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Photocatalysts and Light Absorbers. A photocatalyst is a material that speeds up a chemical change when light falls on it, without being used up itself, and a light absorber is the part of a device that catches sunlight and turns it into electric charges that can do chemical work. The two are often the same material doing both jobs. The research problem is finding materials that absorb enough sunlight, last in water and light, and are made from abundant elements rather than rare ones. This record specifies the search: material classes, their light absorption, their stability, and their cost. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. Photoelectrochemical Cells

JAH-APHO-000006 · photoelectrochemical cells · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Artificial Photosynthesis Database, catalog item 20 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Photoelectrochemical Cells. A photoelectrochemical cell is a device that uses light falling on a material in a liquid to drive a chemical change, such as splitting water. The cell holds the light absorber in an electrolyte, which is the liquid that lets charged particles move, and wires carry the charges to where the chemistry happens. Its design must balance light catching, charge movement, and chemical speed, all in one wet device. This record specifies the research: cell shapes, electrolytes, wiring, and the sealing that keeps the liquid in and the air out. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. Solar Fuels

JAH-APHO-000007 · solar fuels · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Solar Fuels. A solar fuel is a fuel made using sunlight, such as hydrogen made by splitting water with sunlight, that can be stored and used later. Solar fuels are valuable because fuels can be stored for months, moved by pipe or ship, and used in engines and furnaces that electricity cannot easily serve. This record specifies the research: which fuels to make, how to measure the sunlight-to-fuel efficiency honestly, and how to compare a solar fuel fairly with a fuel made any other way. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. Catalysts for Oxygen Production

JAH-APHO-000008 · catalysts for oxygen production · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Artificial Photosynthesis Database, catalog item 20 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Catalysts for Oxygen Production. Catalysts for oxygen production are the materials that speed up the half of water splitting that releases oxygen, which is the harder half of the reaction because it must move four electrons and four protons at once. Plants use a cluster of manganese and calcium atoms for this job, and researchers copy that cluster and invent new ones from abundant metals. This record specifies the research: catalyst materials, how fast they work, how long they last, and the tests that prove oxygen was really made. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The system identity is fixed: this is a future artificial photosynthesis record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established practice, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The research question this record answers is: Which abundant catalysts can release oxygen fast enough and long enough to split water? A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Semiconductors for Sunlight Capture. A semiconductor is a material that can absorb light and turn it into moving electric charges, and it is the light-catching part of most solar devices. Its band gap, which is the energy a photon of light must carry to free a charge, decides which colours of sunlight it can use: too wide and it wastes the red light, too narrow and it wastes the blue light's extra energy. This record specifies the research: semiconductor materials, their band gaps, their pairing into stacks that catch more colours, and their protection from water. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The research subject covered here is Electron Flow and Charge Separation. Electron flow and charge separation are the heart of artificial photosynthesis: sunlight frees electrons and protons, and the device must pull them apart and send them to opposite sides before they fall back together and waste the energy. Separation is done with electric fields inside the device, with layered materials, and with catalysts that grab the charges quickly. This record specifies the research: how separation is measured, how much energy it costs, and how to keep charges apart long enough to do chemistry. The research subject covered here is Measuring Efficiency: Solar to Fuel. Measuring efficiency means stating, in the same way everywhere, how much of the sunlight falling on a device ends up as chemical energy in its fuel. The honest measure counts all the sunlight, all the fuel actually made, and the energy spent running the device, and it states the test conditions: the light level, the temperature, and the time. Without shared measures, one laboratory's claim cannot be compared with another's. This record specifies the measurement standard and the tests that prove a number. Safety and environmental review are the checks that a solar fuel device harms nobody and nothing: its materials must not poison water or soil, its hydrogen must be handled without leaks, its carbon dioxide must come from a counted source, and its waste must be dealt with. Review is done before building, not after, and it covers the whole life of the device from making to disposal. This record specifies the review: hazards, safeguards, waste handling, and the record that proves each check was passed. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is The Future of Solar Fuels. The future of solar fuels is the road from laboratory devices to working fuel plants: better materials, longer lifetimes, larger panels, lower costs, and the first plants that make fuel at a price people can pay. The road is long and the field is honest about it: no device today runs at the scale and cost of a working plant, and the horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs. This record specifies the road: the milestones, the measurements that prove each one, and the honest statement of what is still missing. The maturity label for this whole database is E4 Experimental. Science proof: The science proof rests on established practice and documented research, not on a new discovery. Organized knowledge, structured storage, indexed search, and automated testing are the standard methods of software engineering and information retrieval, which is the study of how to find the right records among many. The subject science itself — the physics, chemistry, and engineering behind What Artificial Photosynthesis Is — is documented in the sources cited in this record, and this record states clearly where that science is settled and where it is still open research. A specification that names its parts, a database that stores its records in one shape, a search that returns records with their provenance, a generator that is seeded so its output is repeatable, and a test suite that checks behavior by running it are each established practice, and this system composes them without claiming a new law of nature. Nothing in this record claims a result that was not computed, and nothing here claims that a design is a finished deployed system. The maturity label E4 Experimental is the honest statement of how far the subject has actually come.

Scientific equation: validation = executed demo + executed program + round-trip code + fetched sources + passed generator gates + stated maturity

Proof sketch: The proof sketch follows the validation chain: the demo is executed and its output is captured, the program is executed and reports a deterministic match, the JCC code is recomputed from the record and compared, every cited source web address is fetched and must answer HTTP 200, the maturity label is checked against the pair's label, and the generator is run with a fixed seed twice and must produce identical output. 5. How It Works — Construction Workflow

What Artificial Photosynthesis Is

JAH-APHO-000001 · artificial photosynthesis identity · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. How can sunlight be turned into a storable fuel as reliably as a plant turns it into sugar? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Sunlight cannot be stored as light, so a system that turns it into a storable fuel would let sunshine be kept and used on demand. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of What Artificial Photosynthesis Is as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers solar-to-chemical energy research as one subject, from the plant model to the finished fuel; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; devices are measured the same way everywhere: solar-to-fuel efficiency, stability, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Sunlight, which is the light from the sun that powers the whole process; Fuel, which is a chemical that stores energy and releases it when used; Water splitting, which is breaking water into hydrogen and oxygen using energy; Carbon dioxide, which is the gas in the air that can be turned into a fuel. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future artificial photosynthesis record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are solar to fuel efficiency percent 10, operating hours 1000, stability days 30, device area sq cm 25, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Testing and validation criteria. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group B set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, every maturity label, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present with its plain-words definition; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. Evidence and development status. The evidence label for this public record is documented, which means it is built from fetched public records, and its maturity label is E4 Experimental. The demo figures for this record are solar to fuel efficiency percent 10, operating hours 1000, stability days 30, device area sq cm 25, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it. Future expansion opportunities. The next work after this record is to deepen the neighbouring subjects it links to, to add records for the parts of the subject this record names but does not yet cover in full, and to re-fetch every source on a later date and record what changed, so the specification stays honest as the research moves.

When it applies: This record applies whenever the Master AI (artificial intelligence) needs to research, find, or check a future artificial photosynthesis record: it gives the identity, the complete specification, the database structure, the search and retrieval rules, the working code, the generator, the website interface, the documentation shape, the demonstration, the automated tests, the validation results, the version history, the source provenance, the maturity label, and the expansion instructions in one place. It connects to the other catalog types as follows: Advanced Energy Storage Database (catalog item 19), whose storage holds the fuels this research makes; Future Energy Systems Laboratory (catalog item 55), which tests new generation and storage together; Climate Restoration Research Database (catalog item 36), which needs solar fuels that cut carbon dioxide. The connection rule from the catalog is that one type feeds the next: a database can supply a website, the website can use a generator, and the generator can be registered in the generator database, so the Master AI can discover and reuse all three in future projects. The catalog's convergence rule also applies: one research breakthrough should be usable by other divisions, so a material, a method, or a measurement from this subject is recorded in a form other subjects can reuse.

Verification path: To verify this record, re-read the specification requirements in the method and check each one against the record; run the demo and confirm its output matches the expected output exactly and reports Deterministic: MATCH; run the program and confirm it reports a deterministic match; recompute the JCC code from the record and confirm it round-trips; fetch every cited source web address and confirm it answers HTTP 200; confirm the maturity label is E4 Experimental with its plain-words definition; run the pair generator with seed 42 twice and confirm identical output; and confirm the terms glossary defines every term the record uses in plain words.

The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. How can a device split water with sunlight efficiently enough, and for long enough, to matter? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Hydrogen made from sunlight and water would be a clean storable fuel, but the device must catch light and drive chemistry at once. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers solar-to-chemical energy research as one subject, from the plant model to the finished fuel; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; devices are measured the same way everywhere: solar-to-fuel efficiency, stability, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Hydrogen, which is the lightest element, a gas that releases energy when burned or used in a fuel cell; Oxygen, which is the gas released when water is split, the same gas living things breathe; Catalyst, which is a material that speeds up a chemical change without being used up; Reaction, which is a chemical change that turns starting materials into products. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future artificial photosynthesis record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. Working source code. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Prototype or simulation plan. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Solar Fuels

JAH-APHO-000007 · solar fuels · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. Which solar fuels are worth making, and how is their true efficiency measured? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Sunlight is plentiful but cannot be stored or shipped, while a fuel made from it can do both. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Semiconductors for Sunlight Capture

JAH-APHO-000010 · semiconductors for sunlight capture · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. Which semiconductors, alone or stacked, catch the most sunlight without rotting in water? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. A light catcher that uses only part of the sunlight wastes the rest, and one that rots in water cannot work in a wet cell. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Semiconductor, which is a material that absorbs light and turns it into moving electric charges; Band gap, which is the energy a photon must carry to free a charge in a semiconductor; Photon, which is one packet of light energy; Stack, which is layers of semiconductors that together catch more colours of sunlight. Measuring Efficiency: Solar to Fuel

JAH-APHO-000013 · measuring efficiency solar to fuel · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. Which shared measures prove a device's true solar-to-fuel efficiency? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Efficiency claims without shared measures and stated test conditions cannot be compared, and uncomparable claims cannot be trusted. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Measuring Efficiency: Solar to Fuel as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers solar-to-chemical energy research as one subject, from the plant model to the finished fuel; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; devices are measured the same way everywhere: solar-to-fuel efficiency, stability, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Efficiency, which is the share of sunlight energy that ends up in the fuel; Solar-to-fuel, which is the path from sunlight to stored chemical energy; Test conditions, which is the stated light level, temperature, and time of a measurement; Fuel made, which is the amount of fuel actually produced and measured, not assumed. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future artificial photosynthesis record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are solar to fuel efficiency percent 10, operating hours 2000, stability days 90, device area sq cm 25, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Carbon capture, which is gathering carbon dioxide from air or from a smokestack; Smokestack, which is the chimney through which a plant releases its exhaust gas; Energy accounting, which is counting all the energy spent and gained across the whole chain; Coupling, which is joining the capture step to the fuel-making device. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future artificial photosynthesis record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are solar to fuel efficiency percent 7, operating hours 1500, stability days 45, device area sq cm 25, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Testing and validation criteria. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group B set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, every maturity label, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present with its plain-words definition; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. Evidence and development status. The evidence label for this public record is documented, which means it is built from fetched public records, and its maturity label is E4 Experimental. The demo figures for this record are solar to fuel efficiency percent 7, operating hours 1500, stability days 45, device area sq cm 25, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it. Fuels from Sunlight and Water

JAH-APHO-000017 · fuels from sunlight and water · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. How can hydrogen from sunlight and water be made, stored, and used with the smallest honest loss? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Hydrogen from sunlight and water is the cleanest solar fuel, but every step from making to using it loses energy. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. Materials for Artificial Photosynthesis

JAH-APHO-000020 · materials for artificial photosynthesis · Maturity: E4 Experimental — E4 means experimental: experiments exist and have produced results, but the work is not yet routine and not yet ready for engineers to build with at full scale. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. Which abundant materials can catch light, drive chemistry, and survive water and light? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Every better solar fuel device starts as a better material, and materials must be proven in cells before they are trusted in panels. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Materials for Artificial Photosynthesis as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers solar-to-chemical energy research as one subject, from the plant model to the finished fuel; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; devices are measured the same way everywhere: solar-to-fuel efficiency, stability, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Material, which is one substance a device is built from, such as a light absorber or a catalyst; Membrane, which is the thin layer that separates parts of a device while letting charged particles pass; Protective layer, which is a coating that shields a material from water and light; Yardstick, which is the shared measures every material is judged by. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future artificial photosynthesis record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are solar to fuel efficiency percent 9, operating hours 2000, stability days 90, device area sq cm 5, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser.
